ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u3	President, President, ladies and gentlemen, I am going as Parliamentary Commissioner to this Hall of Horreo, and I am honoured to be able to present briefly to the House the report of the Valador for 2014. I will do so briefly, for it has already taken place in committee a few days ago, in the Committee on Petitions, where, of course, there has been a rich exchange of ideas. And besides, the report has hung on Valador's website for almost three months. Therefore, I will be – I insist – brief. The participatory democracy that the present reality requires, needs, is necessary, control bodies to exercise a permanent function of oversight of the public authorities. In this sense, an adequate institutional structure is needed in these control bodies, based on balance, transparency, qualification and responsibility. This will strengthen the commitment to citizenship and will provide greater legitimacy to public entities. The figure of the ombudsman – from the valets, in the modern sense – responds to the similar situation, and reveals himself as an organ that improves democratic quality in our societies, deepening equality, reinforcing solidarity, or allowing for increased citizen participation. This is the idea that we try to respond to in the People's Valedor, bringing our efforts closer to the public services of Galicia and good administration to progress more and more in our autonomous community. 2014 was again an intense year in the Valedor, in which this institution carried out a very demanding task, both in the aspect of pedagogy and dissemination of rights and in the resolution of consultations and complaints that came to us. There is no doubt that this body is already a very consolidated institution in our autonomous community, with great visibility and permanent reference to public opinion. That, no doubt, congratulates us. The general competence of the Ombudsman, that is, the guarantee of human rights, is enshrined – as is known – in the control of the principle of good administration. In practice, this usually means that disadvantaged or vulnerable people are the priority object of attention by the Valador. Last year before this House, he was talking about the existence of a series of challenges pending for fundamental rights. I'm not going to repeat those ideas now, but I underline their vigilance. He talked about the interaction between fundamental rights and the digital world, social rights in an environment of crisis, the consequences of multiculturalism, the new environmental demands, the right subject to good administration, etc. With Georg Jellinek, Leipzig's jurist, fundamental rights theory received an admirable systematic, distinguishing between different states of the person. Now, in the 21st century, we must continue to advance this theory of fundamental rights to adapt it to new needs and thus overcome those challenges pending for rights. In this qualified mission it must be emphasized that social rights have to evolve in the phenomenological aspect; that is, in the application dimension, in the dimension of their effectiveness, which necessarily requires progress also in their coerciveness, and collecting the position of Jürgen Habermas, who said that democracy revolves around its transformation and not around the mere accumulation of ideas. The new deliberative frameworks for decision-making show the need to encourage these changes. With regard to the question of figures for 2014 it is necessary to make an initial reference which I have already expressed on other occasions. Rights problems are not quantitative but qualitative; that is, numbers have relative importance. Do this precision, I say something of these numbers, these numbers. The figures for 2014 – I insist – show this intense work of Valador that I had commented on earlier. The people attended by us were more than thirty thousand –30,722 – divided into four groups: people who attend us at present, telephone attention, complaints files and other files – usually consultations –. To these numbers we should add all the rights pedagogy acts we do in the institution. I have to thank publicly for the enormous effort my staff make in this intense work, with a commitment to the Valedor as an institution and a passion for defending people's rights. Also, on this line, I have to recognize the continuing help provided by NGOs and third sector entities, always very sensitive to our requests. On the subject of complaints, summarizing the comparison with other years, it can be said that more people have arrived than in previous years – more than 24,000 claimants – but fewer issues – 2,310 in particular –. This difference in complaints between claimants and matters is explained by the existence of collective complaints. This phenomenon – that of collective complaints, in groups – is persistent in recent times. It's clearly seen that people come to us in a more organized way, using digital technology intensely, which makes that organization easier. This is an example of the new forms of public management, of the public, of a more participatory type. The new society is more demanding and deliberative, and it employs the control organs, like the ombudsman, continuously. I think this is good for democracy. I also want to comment on this issue of the figures that four extraordinary reports were launched in the Valedor in 2014, two that have already been completed today: one of all Spanish defenders on the situation of hospital emergencies in the State, and another, specific to Valador, on transparency and access to information. At the final stage of preparation we have two other reports started last year, one about the accessibility of people with disabilities and one about socio-health care to people with mental illness. In that year 2014 we started a new extraordinary report on the rights of LGBTI people, which we are about to end in a few days. Another issue I would like to mention at the moment is the high collaboration that the public authorities have had with Valador, both in the provision of information and in the fulfilment of the resolutions issued by this Ombudsman. The pitfalls in this cooperation are exceptional, I have criticized them at the moment. The common agenda, on the other hand, is agile and loyal collaboration, which allows a high number of problems for citizenship, and which shows a network and synergistic work among the various institutions that improves the public system. Thank you, therefore, for the collaboration of all these public powers; of all the political signs, really. Our institutional activity focused on the dissemination and pedagogy of rights, which makes it possible to grow a better standard in the culture of human rights in Galicia. In this sense, in 2014 we organized for this purpose day-to-days and training seminars, and developed several campaigns. That is, alongside the processing of complaints, an ombudsman has a qualitatively different role, based on the aforementioned culture of human rights dissemination. For example, a relevant Valador line of work is the one that studies the interaction between teenagers and the Internet in Galicia. A line of work, by the way, which is already known internationally. In this way, we are trying to ensure that young people and young women in Greece make responsible use of technology, other than technology, an instrument of harassment or gender-based violence and that there are no cyberdependence situations. Even so, we act for women's rights. We'll only be an evolved society if we can forever banish discrimination on the basis of gender. This activity is also carried out at international level with the gallo to deal with trafficking in persons for the purpose of sexual exploitation, a problem that persists, unfortunately, in Galicia. Another advocacy activity focuses on defending the rights of the LGBTI collective. This is a new function entrusted to the Valedor by this Parliament, which is aimed at eliminating any kind of discrimination on the grounds of sexuality or how to feel that sexuality. In the same way, we act to promote the principle of transparency. This principle has now become an essential element of current democracy, both in its objective aspect – i.e. the active publicity that the public authorities have to do – and in its subjective aspect – that is, the right of people to access information. Our commitment to transparency is highlighted in our new institutional website, open to citizenship, dynamic, accessible and usable, which is serving as an example both in the Ibero-American Federation of the Ombudsman and in the International Ombudsman Institute. Connected with these issues is the bet on digital technology, which has a strategic dimension in the Valedor that allows us to be more efficient. Finally, we also strongly push for the candidacy of the Sacred Ribeira to the Heritage of Humanity, a distinction that gives UNESCO, as it is known. A group of experts has drawn up, with our coordination, the basic technical report which will be used to submit the nomination, if the competent bodies so consider appropriate, of course. In fact, next week we organize a course in this Peace of Parliament on this issue, the candidacy of the Sacred Ribeira to the Heritage of Humanity. I invite everyone and everyone to take part in this course, if they consider it appropriate. I now refer to some examples of investigations carried out on the basis of the complaints submitted in 2014. It'll only be a small alternative to the issues we've been dealing with, in which we try to help at all times to advance the quality of public services in Galicia. The first of these examples refers to the so-called ground clauses in mortgage loans, i.e. agreements limiting the variability of interest. From a legal-social point of view, ground clauses are an example of abuse of dominant position, unfair because it does not allow the logical evolution of markets to benefit consumers properly. In view of this, it is necessary for banking institutions to show social sensitivity and not take advantage of people by means of these clauses. The jurisprudence of the Supreme Court is clear in the face of information and transparency deficits. The biggest collective complaint of the year 2014 referred precisely to bank abuse, signed nothing more and nothing less than 6,700 people. Similarly, Valador's actions must be underlined in defense of the Greek language. No doubt one of the axes that move this institution is the legal and moral obligation to preserve and promote the language we inherit from our ancestors. Public authorities and society must properly protect and disseminate the Welsh language and leave it in an excellent condition for the future. It seems that we must insist on the use of Greek in interpersonal relationships in the private sphere, in the technological field and in the family nuclei. We'll have to get closer to the population, especially to the newest, German technology. So the promotion of initiatives such as ".gal" domain, rather than we were pioneers, contributes to identifying that newer population with our language. The Valador applauds these and other initiatives that contribute to creating a Welsh digital identity in the virtual world. It is also useful to design audiovisual products, as well as radio content, attractive to citizenship. Valador also works in favour of language at the international level, through the so-called IALC, which is the International Association of Linguistic Commissioners. We request in 2014 our admission to the so-called international association to give a boost to our language. Now we formally integrate that association. Others of our actions affect the relevant subject of rejections. The Valador has been working for years with specific performances to ensure a decent home for all people. In this way, in the face of the challenge of evictions, we articulate multiple and diverse activities with those affected, with the support platforms, with the social services of the Union, with the social services of the municipalities, with the judicial bodies and with the banking institutions. And all this succeeds, because we've always been able to stop the defiance that came to us in the form of a complaint. Even so, we must emphasize our work in favour of the integration of people with disabilities. This is a challenge of democratic quality and moral imperative for all. In this way, we seek to promote the rights of persons with disabilities by promoting their autonomy, mobility and accessibility, access to leisure and culture, sport and quality of life. There were also various issues dealt with that affect minors as a collective: cyberbullyng, poverty, obesity, alcohol and other drugs. These issues affecting minors must be investigated on a permanent basis, that is, they should be lines of continuous work and not just isolated complaints. In this sense, the fight against the risk of social exclusion of the Galician families, especially those who have the least in charge, goes through the highest efforts of administration and society. The variables that can cause these situations are clearly identified. Now what we have to develop is broad spectrum indicators that make it possible to measure child inequality in a multidimensional way. Without quality data, it is not possible to discuss the specific legal and policy measures to be taken or to create follow-up and annual evaluation processes to measure action plans on poverty and inequality. Thus, it seems necessary to intensify support for municipal social services in order to develop programmes directed primarily to homes with minors. In the consumption of alcohol and other drugs by minors, we demand a genuine commitment from mothers and parents, who must take on the obligations of the homeland's power. In addition, public authorities must carry out information and awareness campaigns, monitoring studies and accurate and efficient protocols for such action. In connection with issues already mentioned, we have other actions by this Ombudsman to fight against social exclusion. The real genetic profile of an ombudsman is to help people who find themselves in a vulnerable situation, in a situation of social exclusion. This fight is supposed to affect the intrinsic equality Robert Dahl spoke of to say democracy was the best system. Most social complaints refer in 2014, to delays and denials of social inclusion aid or municipal emergency aid. In many cases we confirm delays, although also in many others who are supposed to be in our intervention, it has led to the final resolution and payment of the benefit. We always call for rapid processing in the resolution of these procedures by the responsible authorities. Indeed, situations of social exclusion or risk require this rapid reaction, because otherwise these social benefits are not useful. The increase in applications for aid has led to the administrations also increasing the budgetary share devoted to them. In the field of the environment, this institution receives various complaints in which we advocate conservationism and a strong idea of sustainability to preserve the rich natural spaces we have in Galicia, our flora and our fauna. In another sense, the numerous complaints related to local corporations seek, above all, in 2014, to overcome obstacles in access to municipal information and documentation. It is important to make progress in this direction so that neighbours become real active subjects of municipal life. On the subject of energy poverty we had to resolve different complaints about it. Energy poverty is a new concept derived from a more demanding society, more aware of its position, more demanding – as I said before –. It's about updating the social state, adapting it to new needs, which is again connected to what it said before the challenges that currently have human rights in the 21st century. To correct these unfavorable effects of rising energy prices, the so-called "electric social bond" – which is state aid – was created. And in addition to this aid, Galicia has established the so-called "social energy bill", which became effective last year. In any event, this institution believes that it is necessary to set social prices or reduced rates subsidised for people in a vulnerable situation which allow them to have the minimum necessary to enjoy dignified and healthy living conditions. In this field we should also ask for the collaboration of energy suppliers, who are sensitive and responsible when producing cuts, for example, in delicate moments – in winter –. We must, of course, demand social awareness and social responsibility in their activities. I'm done. Norberto Bobbio, the Italian master, said that public authorities should seek justice through policies of integration and tolerance. I understand that the Valador is powerfully helping to achieve this goal driven by an institutional commitment of continuous progress and updating in the search for best practices that satisfy all those who approach us for help. Human rights give true epistemological meaning to the functioning of this ombudsman, committed to the progress of society, especially those who need it most because of his vulnerability. Without a doubt, the most unprotected people are the main object of our developments to try to improve their situation, it's a task of giants but it's also of justice. Thank you very much for your attention right now. Thank you very much to all my Commissioners, that is, to all Members, for their cooperation and assistance at all times to this Commissioner. I therefore go up to the guest gallery to listen to their comments. Thank you very much.

As it is also significant that, among the matters dealt with before the Valedoria with the support of a plurality of persons, complaints about evictions, ground clauses and other abusive clauses of banking entities should be raised. On many occasions, we have been discussing the rejections in this Parliament, and the report contains some conclusions that we consider to be outstanding. Inadequate legal changes and measures are recognised. The possibility of accepting the gift paid was insufficient – said the report – because very restrictive economic requirements have been established that leave many affected. As they are also very restrictive – the report is repulsed – the requirements to accommodate the possibility that the launch may be suspended for a period of two years. And there's a clear tip of ears because of the delay in creating a social housing fund, accompanied by the observation that the requirements for access should be flexible. In another order, it seems very illustrative of the social emergency situation that we are experiencing the increase in complaints in the area of social services. Complaints motivated by delays and other problems arising from the care of personal autonomy and the promotion of personal autonomy are now the ones motivated by delays and obstacles in granting Risk and other aid. In some cases, the beneficiaries of the Risk suffer delays of up to one year, a matter which the BNG has repeatedly denounced in this same Parliament. The report points out that delays add up to interpretative doubts to accommodate the aid brought by Law 10/2013. In this sense, I would like to remind you that, at the initiative of the BNG, the agreement has been reached that the administrative silence assumes the automatic granting of the Risk when the legal maximum established to respond to the request is exceeded. But the report shows that it is not being applied, which, together with the abolition of the three-month interim payment guarantee, only aggravates the situation. Ladies and gentlemen, from our point of view, this situation which reflects the report is a demonstration of the absolute lack of the slightest social sensitivity on the part of the current government. According to a recent report by the European Network of Combating Poverty and Social Exclusion, Galicia has over 90,000 people in severe poverty, more than 370,000 people in poverty and nearly 700,000 at risk of poverty. And with this emergency situation it is simply scandalous that these pitfalls and delays in granting aid occur. The report also reports numerous non-compliances in relation to dependency. Stresses the existence of considerable delays in the care programmes and the effectiveness of the services and benefits allocated. Delays that are even more considerable in applications for economic benefits. And he realizes many other problems generated by cutting policies in this area. We are also interested in comments about energy poverty. Energy poverty in Galicia continues to increase despite the paradox that we are a surplus country in electricity production. It is estimated that it already affects 100,000 Greek homes, and the report realises that the number of applicants for the social energy ticket established by the Union is simply ridiculous, only 352. The reason: the conditions for benefit are very restrictive and, in addition, only telematics can be requested, when people who are in a state of energy poverty, as it is obvious, in most cases also have enormous difficulties in accessing new technologies. And besides, it's not enough to provide the income statement, but it's also mandatory to report on municipal social services. The report advocates precautionary measures, such as preventing the cut-off or interruption of the service to persons suffering from this situation. But, as we have already stated in committee, from our point of view it is not enough to appeal only to the social responsibility of companies. It is necessary to adopt shock measures from the political point of view to combat this important social problem. As far as health is concerned, one fact that speaks for itself is that it absorbs more than 25% of the complaints filed by individuals. Most of the complaints are motivated by the deterioration in the quality of public health, and much of them by the impact of the deterioration in health personnel cuts. And speaking of clippings, it's very interesting that the conclusions relating, by setting an example, to the suppression of pediatric surgery services in the University Hospital Complex of Ourense. There's a new shot of ears at the Councillor's office, which has also largely dismissed the Valador's recommendation. Valedoria recommended the continuity of the service, recalling that in every health centre the emergency service is a primary service to ensure immediate health care in an emergency situation, apart from the economic and family disorders that represent the transfer to Vigo, and remembering that we are also talking about minors, users who require special protection. Therefore, the quality appeal cannot be an alibi for dismantling the service. In teaching, among other things, the alert report of the deterioration – and in many cases, genuine degradation – of educational centres, especially those of the highest age, and the lack of coverage of specific educational needs. With regard to public employment, we agree on the importance, as reflected in the report, of limiting timeliness. For this is totally incompatible with the provision of quality public services. In the environment it is clear that, twenty years after the entry into force of the rules on transparency and citizen participation, its application presents important shortcomings, requests for information are not met in time or are not adequately satisfied and the rules on the participation of environmental organisations in the consultation bodies are not complied with. And concerning the Corcoesto mine, the report says textually that the environmental impact declaration would not be valid to reactivate the request; that is, the new procedure should start from scratch, against the thesis that has so far been supported by the Galician Union. Debates and votes on the opinion of the Committee on Agriculture, Food, Glanding and Mountains on the draft legislation on improvement of the agricultural structure of Galicia
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u15	They remain for the full amendment of the Joint Group, the Galician Nationalist Block, the Alternative Left Galician and the Socialist Parliamentary Group. The President of the Commission, Mr Vidal Martín-Sierra López, spoke for the presentation of the Commission's dictum.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u16	Thank you, Madam President. Good morning. Tell me about the draft legislation on improvement of Galicia's agricultural territorial structure. The bill was entered into Parliament's Register on December 2, 2014. The Bureau of Parliament issued its publication in the Official Bulletin of the Galician Parliament and the opening of the deadline for the amendments. Even so, listening to the spokesperson's board, he agreed to attend the 7th Committee on Agriculture, Food, Gloves and Mountains. The amendments to the whole were qualified by the Bureau of the Commission 7th on 12 January 2015, and the partial amendments on 23 March 2015. The parliamentary groups of the Alternative Glaga de Left, the Galego Nationalist Bloc and the Galician Socialists each presented amendments to the whole, which were rejected at the plenary session of February 10, 2015. As for the partial amendments, the People's Parliamentary Group made thirty, the Parliamentary Group of the Socialists of Galicia twenty-five, the Parliamentary Group of the Forty-Fifteenth Left, the Parliamentary Group of the Nationalist Block Galego ninety, and the Joint Parliamentary Group ten. Commission 7a appointed the following speakers: by the People's Group of Galicia, Ms María Isabel García Pacín as the holder and Fr José Antonio Freire Abeijón as alternate; by the Socialists of Galicia, Fr Pablo García García as the holder and Don María Soledad Soneira Tajes as alternate; by the Alternative Gaga de Left, Fr Antón Sánchez García as the holder, and Fr Ramon Vázquez Díaz as alternate; and, finally, by the Group of the Nationalist Block Galego, Ms María Terêsea Paz Franco as the owner and don Ana Belén Pontón Mondelo as alternate. The Constitutive Session of the Presidency took place on 20 April 2015, with the assistance of the Chairman of the 7th Commission. The Presidency held its meeting on 27 April and 4 and 11 May, in which the amendments submitted and the draft text were discussed, and the report was drawn up, which was put to the debate and the vote at the 7th meeting of the Committee on 29 May, and all proposals from the Presidency were ratified. The articulated text of the dictame contains ninety-eight articles, three additional layouts, five transitional layouts and five last ones, and is structured into ten titles. Title I, referring to general provisions, regulates the object of law, the general objectives, the scope of application, the types of land structure improvement processes, and the definitions of terms used throughout their articulation. Title II is dedicated to the regulation of partial restructuring processes of a public character, when the impulse is on the part of the Administration. Title III is dedicated to restructuring by private people, when impulse makes it a group generated expressly to carry it out. Title IV refers to restructuring by permuting with the assumed exchange rate or not in the form of the permuted plots. Title V collects two cases of special processes: for public works and cut mining, whose execution conditions the viability of agricultural holdings. Titles VI and VII contain, respectively, the provisions relating to works inherent in the partial restructuring processes, as well as the effects and the financing and public aid scheme. Title IX sets up areas of intensive action, with a view to increasing their sustainability, competitiveness and integral rural development. And finally, title X collects the sanctioning regime. So far, the work of the 7th Commission, which I am presiding over. It is therefore necessary to complete the legislative procedure today by submitting its ruling to plenary consideration. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u17	Thank you very much. The turn of parliamentary groups to set their position on the dictatorship and, if so, defend the amendments maintained. For the Joint Group, you have the floor to Mrs. Martínez.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u18	Well, I'll start my speech by defending my Amendment No 10, adding to the explanatory statement of three new paragraphs with the following text: "The reduction in the number of farms that have occurred because of the disappearance of dairy cows has led to the current situation of cows without land and land without cows. Under the pretext of modernization to adapt to the milk quotas imposed by the Treaty of Accession to the Common Market, the vast majority of farms have disappeared and others have been pushed into debt to acquire quota and adopt a model of food based on purchase and production, not on land production. For the purposes of the predominant agricultural policy in the last thirty years, they have ruined the countryside, but they have also seriously damaged the trade of the villages. But the most serious thing was the liquidation of the genuine food production system, efficient and environmentally respectful, instead of promoting a genuine modernization that should focus on further improving land production." So we started from an absolute lack of self-criticism and critical analysis of the actions that brought us to the present situation of our farm. The biggest problem in this bill is the same as the general flaw that has almost all the normative work of the People's Party, and which is manifested every year in the Budgets Act: there is a permanent absence of a strategic project for the country and for everyone and each of the productive sectors about which he says he wants to act; it comes from a false and very partial diagnosis of the sectors about which he intends to act; and when for bloody situations there are lines of action, they have so little economic support that they result in smoke, propaganda or fireworks. Here, too, the Galician Land Structure Improvement Bill is not part of a diagnosis of the present situation of agriculture and the causes of the decline in recent decades in which the PP ruled the Union with its two brief breaths into the country. A condemned farm since the three parties of the 78 regime – Popular Party, Socialist Party and Communist Party – came upon and defended an entry into the common market in ruinous conditions for our farming. The peasants and farmers were right when they shouted, "European Common Market, ruin of the Greek people," in the mobilizations of the 1980s and 1990s. The common market has assumed, yes!, to integrate almost a monopoly of food into European multinationals. Dozens of thousands of agricultural holdings disappeared because peasants were literally driven out by dairy quotas. The abandonment of the countryside did not fall from the sky, nor was it the fruit of the trawling of a fiery industry or trade as it happened in other latitudes. The increase in fires is not a consequence of any curse either. The aging of the rural population, too. And without being aware of the causes that brought us to this situation of true social, ecological and economic urgency of the Galician agriturismo, we can hardly remedy anything. I am stopped at the liquidation of the dairy sector because of the qualitative importance and because we are seeing the situation in which it remains worse. The policies of the Union are oriented in the last twenty-five years to conceal the liquidation of the dairy sector and agriculture, with modernization policies that have resulted only in the concentration of production on a small number of insufficient land-based farms and increasingly dependent on the purchase of food for livestock. Besides, they had to commit themselves to buy quota, and now they feel logically cheated and in many cases at risk of disappearance. At good hours green sleeves with the improvement of Galicia's agricultural territorial structure for many sheep and sheep! First, they liquidated the little ones, not modern ones, and now they let those who have modernized themselves die, following the leadership of the People's Party. In the distribution of direct aid and rural development, contrary to the triumphal tone of the Government of the United States, there are worrying objectives for Galicia. Galicia is charging only 3% of the direct CAP aid to Spain, when it is around 6% of agricultural production and between 8% and 9% of farms and agricultural employment. If in the previous period of the CAP, the Galician Rural Development Programme has counted 1,500 million euros, in the period 2014-2020, according to the information of the Union itself, it will have 1,187 million euros, more than 300 million falls, which is the same: 21% less. So farmers and farmers, farmers and farmers have done their homework, but now they are faced with problems that do not depend on them, such as the problem of access to land to have a territorial base that allows them to reduce the purchase of thought. The end of the dairy quota endangers current farms, which a responsible government would have to support, and opens up the opportunity to recover small farms that can be a source of self-employment for people expelled from other sectors due to the economic crisis. The professor who holds the Department of Applied Economics of the University of Santiago de Compostela, Edelmiro López Iglesias, regarding this problem, says that Galicia needs "to make less dependent holdings of food for cattle and more based on forage production, and that means putting her teeth on the problem of land. Consolidate a transformation industry in Galicia and organize the relationship in the chain of production between gourds, industries and distribution chains that today govern the law of the jungle." We need to think about the improvement of Galicia's agricultural territorial structure within a strategic plan for agriculture, and not make a law to kill the active partial concentrations and try to raise funds by hardening the sanctioning regime. If we think that agriculture must, first of all, be the main source of food for our citizens, then it's when it makes sense to talk about recovering useful agricultural land, but it also requires a Greek agroindustrial sector and not the flaying of dairy food. Today we have a powerful dairy sector in production, with farms that are viable in the medium and long term in the European Union, but we have a milk industry in Galicia that is usually weak, and that of German capital even more. Galicia produces 40% of state milk, the dairy industry located in Galicia does not reach 10% in terms of employment and added value on the state set, and that of German capital is still in the third part. That is, by producing 40% of state milk, the German capital industry accounts for 3% of the state. And if there isn't an industry that completes the productive chain, the German sector will remain vulnerable. It's about the marketing of short circuits, and all that's known and you in the People's Party have often seen without ever listening. When the German agriturismo becomes the main source of food, many of the ecological dangers such as fires will decrease, the rural population will recover, the trade of the villages will be a key sector for sustainable economic recovery. In addition, the text of the bill misses the most important, which is tax incentives. It would also regulate the protection of the rights of landowners, and in many cases they are properties of emigrants or children and grandchildren of emigrants, of whom the PP remembers only to bring a bunch of votes every four years. And if it's not possible to locate the owners, let those lands pass to the parishes. I therefore maintain the amendments to the draft legislation on land-based land-based improvement in Galicia, and I regret that Galicia today loses the great opportunity to reach a parliamentary agreement that would provide for the recovery of the Greek agricultural sector and the creation of a Galician agri-food industry. Amendment 1 seeks to give priority to family farms, cooperative forms and communal property, as I consider it essential to establish rural populations, ensure quality food production and improve territorial cohesion. I understand that the People's Party's refusal to accept this amendment is a consequence of its commitment to hand over our agriculture to multinationals in the sector, so that they continue to exploit our farmers and farmers. The wording of Article 1 simply speaks of prioritizing family holdings, cooperative forms and communal property, in order to achieve the general objectives set out in Article 2. Amendments 3 and 4 seek to remove in Article 56(4) and 57(6) the definition of access to specific aids for the registration of real estate. The text of the bill speaks of whether these will be the case, without clarifying when the case will be or when it will not be the case. An indefinition which, in addition to being based on the lack of transparency in the performance of the Administration, creates legal insecurity and defence in order to demand its establishment or application. And the same is true in relation to Amendment No 5 of Article 58.1, which eliminates the inconcretion that a special process of partial restructuring may be promoted, or is promoted or not promoted. We are talking about the consequences of the execution of a public work or the exploitation of a miner knife and how to mitigate the harmful effects on the structure of the agricultural holdings in the affected area. Therefore, I believe that whenever agricultural holdings are affected by public works or mining, they should be restructured if they so request – as set out in Amendment No 6 – at least 50 per cent of the persons holding agricultural holdings listed in the different official registers and active in the area directly affected by them. Amendment 7 seeks to ensure that prior to the occupation of the land the project promoter and execution will present the financial commitment to ensure that the costs of partial restructuring are sufficiently paid. It is not enough that there is a financial commitment, but it must ensure cost coverage. No promoter should be able to initiate the execution of a work without the full financial guarantee to cover all the costs arising from that action. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u68	Thank you, President. Good morning, gentlemen. First of all, I would like to thank and greet the representatives of the Galaga Association of Dietists and Nutritionists who are in the rostrum of this Chamber. For four years now, this German association has filed a request for the creation of its professional school. This request was submitted to public information without any allegations opposing the processing of the draft law. There was therefore no questioning of the public interest character of that school; public interest which now, as you will see, reject opposition groups by keeping their amendments. The processing of this preliminary draft – as has already been said here – has suffered a delay motivated by the attempt to accompany its approval by the Government of the State, a new draft bill of professional services and schools, in compliance with the transitional provision for the fourth of Law 25/2009, of December 23. This preliminary draft should include among its functions defining which professions are compulsory, which would allow us to adopt in Galicia texts of school creation laws already adapted to the requirements of the new state legislation. As after the elaboration of several versions of the preliminary draft did not prosper its approval, it was therefore that the Government of the United States brought to this Parliament the draft law on the creation of the Official College of Dietists-Nutritionists of Galicia. That's why I was surprised by some of the opposition groups' statements about the legislative activity of our Government and the pressure the People's Parliamentary Group had to pass this law. I think, ladies and gentlemen, those statements are not fair. In the State there are already ten official schools of dietists-nutritionists in various autonomous communities, and even – here mentioned the ESC report – approved the General Courts of Law 19/2014, of the creation of the General Council of Professional Colleges of Dietists-Nutritionists of Spain, a law that, although they cannot believe it, has relied on the unanimous support of all parliamentary groups of the Congress of Deputies. You see, ladies and gentlemen, you see, members of the association, quite the opposite here, in the Galician Parliament. This profession has a clear and fundamental impact on public health. Good eating habits and proper nutrition substantially improve people's quality of life, and they are also decisive in the prevention and treatment of many pathologies. We have to separate the population from the miracle diets and poor diet solutions and scientific evidence that circulate on social networks and many other media. We have seen these days, unfortunately, the severity of pseudo-scientific information – in this case I'm referring to the anti-cunation groups – that keep a child in an ICU in Catalonia with a serious diphtheria painting. The bill states that the collection will be compulsory when it establishes a state law. Such drafting of the article was adopted on the express recommendation of the General Legal Counsel of Galicia, as can be verified in the draft law file. The reason for this recommendation is that in 2009 the Central Government – from which the Socialist Party – has moved the amendment of article 3.2 of the Basic Law on Professional Schools to establish that it will be the Central Government who will develop a bill that defines the professions that will require compulsory college. The Constitutional Court itself has already annulled a number of autonomous precepts in recent years, which sought to regulate the compulsoryity or not of the collection. In any case, it is expressly clear, with the wording contained in the Greek text, that the collection will be voluntary in Galicia to exercise the profession of dietitian-nutritionist until the future state school law decides on the subject. That is why we Members and Members of the Socialist Group do not understand their amendments under Article 4 of this law. In recent years, 2013 and 2014, the laws of Andalusia, Murcia and Castile-Leon have been approved, all by unanimity of the People's Party, Socialist Party and United Left. With regard to volunteering, those laws say the following. Andalusia: "It will not require inclusion in the school unless a state law so provides." Murcia: "In accordance with article 3.2 of Law 2/1974. on 13 February, on professional colleges, in the wording given by Law 25/2009 of 22 December, the obligation on collegial incorporation will only proceed when a state law establishes a basic law." Castela-Leon: "You would need to join the school if a state law so establishes." As has been exposed in the interventions of the groups, the main schools for unanimous approval of this law, in addition to the volunteerity of the collection, of which we have already spoken, is the public interest in the creation of school and the activity of dietists-nutritionists and the reference expressed in the project to Law 7/2004, the Greek Law for equality of women and men, and the Organic Law 3/2007, for the effective equality of women and men, and also strict parity in the management commission. Other amendments that reject our group have to do with the Socialist Group's mention of the structure and democratic functioning of schools, when this is a constitutional article. Article 36 of the Constitution reads: "The internal structure and functioning of schools must be democratic." Of course, in the laws of the 2009 professional schools when the Bipartite ruled, the reference to the democratization and democratic functioning of the schools does not appear at all. There are some BNG amendments, which are 4 and 10, which we cannot accept because we follow the board's legal technical guidelines, which say that it cannot be a transitional provision that ceases to be effective when applied once. Another amendment by the Socialist Group on the collection of professional persons associated with the Public Administration must also refuse it because we consider that it exceeds the competence of the Autonomous Community, and there are different judgments by the Constitutional Court that have referred to the competence to force or exempt the collection strictly state, as is required by the amendment of the Socialist Government of the year 2009 of the Law on Professional Schools. Amendment No. 7 of the AGE Group rejected it because we believe that the general conditions for the approval of the statutes of schools are already clearly defined in the Law on Professional Schools of Galicia, in Law No. 11/2001, and we do not find it advisable to specify specificities in this law, in the law on the creation of this school. We think the right thing is that they are all based on a global rule, because otherwise, in the event that the general procedure regulated by Law 11/2001 had changed, this school would be subject to special regulation. With regard to Amendment No. 2 of the Socialist Group, with regard to the addition of a new article on gender equality and balanced composition, we have to say the same thing. She doesn't regulate or school, she just creates it. School regulation is contained in Law 11/2001. Paragraphs 1 and 2 of the aforementioned amendment on discrimination on grounds of sex, inequality in the treatment of opportunities or rights and duties between women and men in college, and on the nullity of any agreement by the school government bodies that violates laws 7/2004 and 3/2007, seem to us absolutely redundant. There could be no other way to apply to professional schools the laws cited about equality of women and men. In short, as I had already commented in the entire debate of the bill, I was surprised by such a large number of amendments. A total of 31 for a simple text that apparently did not lend itself to large discrepancies. The People's Group accepted 4 and traded 1 of the 13 amendments from the BNG Group, which had withdrawn 1 and remained in 12. The AGE Group presented 7, three accepted and four refused. The socialists presented six, accepted one, transaction one, and rejected four. And from the Joint Group – which had already been the author of an amendment to the whole, as stated here – we could not accept any; one of them referred to the express volunteering of the collection, and the other two deleted paragraphs in the explanatory statement relating to the public interest of this law. And what amendments do we accept? Indeed, we accept the revision of the sexist language and the correction of the text. Amendment 3 of the Nationalist Block Galego partly, because it clarifys the abilities of the school; Amendment 5 of the BNG, where specific reference was made to the new name of the enabling qualification between graduates and graduates. Two AGE amendments could be included in the BNG correction for sexist language. And we also accept 6 of the AGE and 9 of the BNG, which best profiles the transitional work of the management commission that will act on a pre-school basis. They are therefore a total of ten amendments or accepted or traded, almost all of which were acceptable, because the rest of them either violated basic legislation or were either contradictory or redundant with the content of the law. We regret the refusal to vote affirmatively by opposition groups a law of creating such an important professional school for the public health of our citizens. ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u81	You have the floor, for the presentation of the bill, Mr José Luis Méndez Romeo, of the Parliamentary Group of the Socialists of Galicia.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u82	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, modern political doctrine is considering that the main function of parliaments, of legislative chambers, is the function of control over the executive to the detriment of the traditional legislative function, which today is almost monopolistic by the executive, at least in the continental parliamentary system that is closest to us. In a famous work on the institutions of the modern State of Philip Petttit, a well-known American academic, studies and defends that the camera is a place of discussion including and interactive, the place where representatives should argue about the public good or shared interests, avoiding arguing – says the author partisanly or carelessly. Pettit, for example, puts emphasis on institutions that usually here, in parliamentary work, are a little faded. For example, the role of parliamentary committees, where they tell us that when they use public hearings, when they use the negative selection of candidates, dictatorships, appointments, the hearing of social interests, they become real forums that educate citizens in modern political practice. We have not come so far in this Parliament, but it is clear that Pettit's reflection, even from political theory, is pointing to some of the eves of current parliamentaryism and also a possible path to the future. The reform of the Rules of Procedure is a classic of parliamentary life, it is at the level of the abolition of parliaments and the reform of the Senate; that is, in that kind of debate over which there are enough statements, a lot of documentation and, however, no practical position to modify the status quo. It could be said that in practice these are matters on which there is a tacit consensus. In the area of the Galician Parliament, the long-term stability of the last legislation, which is always made up of three parliamentary groups, has set up a virtually unchanged system of operation. At present, with five groups, parliamentary positions designed at another time are being strained by recurring debates about regulatory problems. The Socialist Group has on many occasions defended the need for flexible frameworks for the functioning of political life and has given its support to all timely measures aimed at promoting the agility of debates and the climate of coexistence within the House, but time is making it clear that the regulation needs alterations, which between its 166 articles, 8 complementary provisions and two dozen interpretative agreements, a part of them no longer fulfil its objective. Other parliamentary groups would probably add other articles to the school we present today. We are open to considering them under the premise that reform is relatively urgent. This proposal has now been drawn up with this perspective, in order to encourage that the next session may prevent repetitions of debates and situations which have been judged anachronistic or overcome. I would like to recall that the current regulation was adopted initially in June 1982, there are 33 years, with a broad consensus of 90% of the House. I note for the small history of that vote, supported by the groups of UCDs, Socialists and People's Alliance, the vote in favour of who was then a Member and today President of the Government of Spain, Mr Rajoy. Then communist and nationalist groups abstained. However, the current articulation is later, from 1983, and was drawn up on the basis of proposals from the various parliamentary groups to reach a consensus of 96%. The 1993 partial reform had only the support of the People's Group. And another partial reform, driven by the Socialist Group the following year, achieved unanimity. Even in the past legislation, with the exclusive votes of the majority, a timely change was adopted to bring political participation into line with motherhood or paternity situations. The position of the Socialist Group over these 33 years has always been constant in favour of the consensus reached on this matter, which has always achieved a very broad support, and contrary to unilateral regulatory reforms, however much they had the support of the majority group. We defend on this subject, as in so many others, that the basic laws of autonomy must have a strengthened consensus. That is why we want to achieve a broad consensus again today. We are debating a proposal for the Socialist Group's law, but with the aim of the resulting law being a House law as a whole. For the same reason, it would be very negative that, if taken into consideration today, it would soon be substantially emptied of content. We are aware that it would be preferable to make up a joint Forum on the part of all groups. So we proposed last year in resolution No. 38 of the last debate on the state of autonomy, which was rejected by the majority popular. A proposal similar to the initiative of the BNG Group was subsequently rejected. Even in the Non-Permanent Commission against Corruption, there were some issues that would affect the functioning of the Regulation, but, as you know, that commission was paralysed by the majority group's decision. And more recently, even, the president of the board, in an extraordinary plenary last February about corruption, defended certain measures similar to those proposed today. We propose two types of changes in the debate that we open today. On the one hand, matters relating to the improvement of political debate and the role of control. Secondly, procedural aspects of the operation of the House. As for the first, the improvement of political debate and the search for a debate of more rigor and height, we include: Possibly establish investigative committees at the request of 1/3 of the House. By taking a little of Philip Pettit's reflection, the presence in the research committees of civil society, experts, officials, this contrast of opinions has an extraordinary value for the society we represent here, and in fact represents a true parliament beyond walls. Faced with the current rigidity of the exclusive debate on proposals and motions, it would mean bringing civil society into the core of parliamentary activity, which is control and control – as I said – of the Executive. We also propose to enrich the processing of bills through hearings to high offices and especially affected social entities, implicitly recognizing that the laws are drafted by the Government and that it is only for the House to perfect them, but assuming that the current unproductive procedure must be passed on to a public debate with society. I stress that a hearing procedure in the processing of bills is of no value to the balance of the majority and minority, and therefore does not question the stability of the Government or the fulfilment of its legislative program. We also propose the mandatory presence of the members of the Government when requests for documentation from groups and Members cannot be met in the course of Article 9. I must remember that, today, only in this legislation, and only as far as the Socialist Group is concerned, there are 400 unmet requests for documentation. And I must underline the practice of other cameras. In the Congress of Deputies, for example, members of the House are usually made available to complete records of matters that are required by the various groups. We also propose strengthening the role of the Committee on Petitions, so that it will be one of the areas in which maximum citizen participation is to be verified. These are some of the measures we propose, which would overcome the current rigid conception of the majority and minority, with full reproduction of themes and proposals already seen in the Commission. We propose to revitalize and boost the general political debate, which is the real reason for this House. As far as matters of improvement of the established procedures are concerned, we propose, among other things, to extend the sessions, both from September and January, so that we may decrease, from the current 90 days of inequitable period, to 40 days of that period. We intend to deepen and, at the same time, to speed up the budgetary debate. Firstly, by strengthening the mandatory appearance not only of the members of the Government, but of all the holders of the different sections which include the budget. It is incomprehensible that many of them are authentic strangers throughout the legislation in this House. In parallel, and as a counterpart, we propose to speed up the debate in plenary, avoiding the tedious repetition of what has already been discussed in the Commission. We also propose to shorten the processing periods, to prevent an interpellation from having to be presented three or four weeks in advance, and a question to the president nine days in advance. And we also include measures on transparency in the Members' and Members' regime. Following this socialist initiative, a bill for the reform of the regulation was tabled by the People's Group, which, as stated in its explanatory statement, is a simple reform. In fact, it only aims to include the transparency portal, something which is required by state legislation; to modify the deadline of one of the sessions; to simplify the interpellations; and to restrict the questions to the President on a restrictive basis. Ladies and gentlemen, this group is aware of its relative position in the current composition of the House, but it is also aware of its responsibility. We have traditionally held a position with regard to the Rules of Procedure and also to the customary uses of the House, but that does not prevent us from defending a firm position of improvement in the political debate with initiatives like the one we are presenting today, or with initiatives such as those presented in the general policy debate, in the impetus of the Committee on the Prevention of Corruption or in other bills. This proposal is constructive, ambitious and also attentive to the evolution of contemporary parliamentaryism. There is a window of opportunity in this debate: the hope of overcoming the traditional division between government and opposition in a common arena, to get a more representative institution. I want to ensure, in that sense, that if this proposal is taken into consideration today, this group will maintain a constructive position as far as possible, knowing that the architecture of the institutions is rarely constructed ex novo and that it usually takes place through successive approximations; with regard to the work done by our ancestors in this House, but also with the responsibility of being obliged to propose improvements and broaden the participation framework of citizens. Ladies and gentlemen, this is in summary what we propose in this modest bill that we would like you to have an axillary process in order to come into force, if there is consensus, in the next session. I remember that the debates on evasa, on the limits, of the current regulation have been truly overwhelmingly reproduced, and there is the Journal of Sessions to check it out. And therefore the difficulties and limits are omnipresent for all parliamentary groups. I hope that with the contributions of all of us, we can enrich this initial text and, indeed, come to have a regulation, but for the next 33 years, at least if for the next legislation, which is what everyone commits to us all. Nothing else and thank you very much.

Parliaments, formally, are the depositors of people's sovereignty, but that's wet paper when, at the time of truth, decisions come to us from Madrid or Brussels, or when the governments of the People's Party who actually command Ibex 35. But beyond this reflection, the crisis of parliamentaryism also gives rise to other causes. For example, an annoyance operation, which prevents further debates with the necessary agility on matters that have often been taking the attention of the street or the debate on networks and the media for many days. Is it, for example, welcome that the questions to the President should be recorded nine days in advance of their debate in this House? So we are in favour of lowering the quorum so that investigation commission can be established. We also agree with other measures, such as those aimed at strengthening transparency; to clarify more thoroughly the duties of Members; to promote the appearances of the members of the Government and the obligation to provide information; to make the time available for groups to register their initiatives and they are ready for their debate in the House; the proposal to extend sessions; or measures to promote citizen participation, however much we doubt that the most recent channel is the current Committee on Petitions. We would be more in favour of a citizen participation commission in this Parliament, so that citizens and social groups can come directly to this House and move their proposals and reflections, and we are also in favour of accompanying this measure in forms of direct democracy, direct participation, such as the Parliamentary Group of the BNG has been proposing since the beginning of this legislation. But other measures, on the other hand, we believe that at least they are questionable. For example, the changes that are proposed to be introduced into the ordering of the general policy debate on the state of the Greek nation. I am referring to the fact that the motions for resolutions have to be recorded at the latest 30 minutes after the debate with the President has taken place; that the debate with the President and the debate on the motions for resolutions should not be on differentiated agendas. And I am also referring to your proposal, gentlemen of the Socialist Group, that the intervention of parliamentary groups in this debate is ordered by order of greater or lesser. Think first that politics is changing, which today is first, tomorrow may be the last. But they also think that, with the wording that you propose and with the current composition of the House, after the intervention of the president, the first group to intervene would be the People's Group. But, above all, we strongly disagree with some proposals that are in line with introducing criteria of greater proportionality in the initiative capacity of the groups. All groups must have the same rights, the same own-initiative capacity; proportionality is already established when voting. We believe that the reform of the Rules of Procedure must be used precisely to correct the deficits in democracy that exist in this House; for example, to correct situations like those experienced in the last ordinary plenary session, where, for the second time in this session, a parliamentary group has been deprived of the ability to ask the President. Therefore, if this proposal is accepted, I announce that the Galician Nationalist Block will present amendments to all these aspects. But as in any case what we are voting on today is the admission of this bill, not the final text, we are not going to hinder voting against this proposal so that it can be taken into consideration, as long as there are aspects of those we radically disagree with. Because, ladies and gentlemen of the Socialist Group, I reiterate: reform of the Rules of Procedure, yes, but not so. That's the position of the BNG Parliamentary Group. That is why I announce that we shall abstain from voting on this bill. Thank you very much.

They requested an additional plenary session. As you will understand, there is no one who understands this, especially when, in addition, we are far exceeding – very broadly – the regulation which must be the control shift, the control times that have been established by that Regulation since its origins in the 1980s. So we don't have any complex to defend what this House is doing and how this House is working, because the opposition to the Government has never had as much initiative and it has so much control capacity as in this legislation. It is true that there is a fundamental discrepancy over proportionality. It is true that the Constitutional Court has clearly pronounced itself in various judgments which state that equality between parliamentary groups cannot mean that all groups, regardless of their number of members, must have the same position in all proceedings of the House, because that would be at the expense of equality between Members; and since there are parliamentary groups with figures of different seats, without any nuance, recognize without any equality between groups, it would be a serious breakdown not only of equality between Members, but also of the very existence of democracy. That's what the Constitutional Court says. We know there's this fundamental conflict, but the truth is, things have changed. The fact is that the Socialist Party had abstained at its own time and is now presenting a bill. It is true that some of the aspects of that bill we share. So what we are doing is, first of all, regret to have reached this point; it was not because the People's Group did not try to reach agreement on the right of initiative at the plenary sessions and on the committees that we are in this situation. Secondly, it is true that the situation is going back to a certain unsufferable point by the duration of the plenary, which is totally anomalous in comparative law; and we have even heard the members of the opposition groups, in relation to municipal plenarys, saying that they cannot be done full marathons. The international division of work is today that some countries specialize in winning and others lose. The mode of production and the class structure of each place were successively determined from outside by its incorporation into the universal gear of capitalism. And each one was assigned a function, always for the benefit of the system and the metropolis. For those who perceive history as a competition, the delay and misery of certain places and countries are nothing but the result of their own failure. But reality shows that those who win get rich and progress because of what these countries, in this case Galicia, have lost, lost and will lose. In my opinion, this is what is happening in the Courel: converted into market goods by the belts of the Courel saw, they feed the multinational allies. There will be nothing left in the greenhouse, only the holes in the excavations; and small isolated villages, impoverished, incommunicado, decapitalized and with very serious infrastructure and services problems will be unattractive so that people bet on having a project of life in them. Thus, the villages of Courel will lose population until they reach the phantom figure to which they are condemned by abandonment; they will destroy everything they want and will inflexibly condemn to emigration and poverty the inhabitants of this area according to supposed escalmatory cutting progress that is alien. Centuries of understanding between humans and their environment, which have generated a singular and exemplary environment, will disappear and there will be no remedy. I'm sorry I'm being apocalyptic, but that's how I feel. You are told, ladies and gentlemen – and the counsellor also said in the interpellation – that Coruel already has the maximum protection. True, it will not be by protection figures: Natura 2000 Network, Place of Community Importance, Area of Special Protection of Natural Values, Landscape Painting or Point of Xeological Interest of International Importance. But all these protection figures are not going to stop Coruel from depopulation or improving the living conditions or the services of the area, because no funding is provided; only restrictions, they have all the restrictions of maximum protection that constrain the agricultural work in the three best villages in the surrounding area, without getting any benefit from supporting them. They do not enjoy the advantages of promotion or the maximum budgets that would give them the most known protection figure, which is the park figure, in this case the natural park. But to declare a natural park the Courel saw must – and immediately – paralyze the concessions of new mining and wind farms in the Courel; cancel the authorization of the wind farm of the Oribio saw, in the municipality of Triacastela, in space of the Natura Network The Ancares-The Courel, because, although it has not been built, it is already approved; cancel the two new concessions provided for in the Trap saw and in the Rainforest, that take part in the Council of Courel; and eliminate the forest investments to plant pines, pyrophytic plants and allies, and that no investment can be considered according to the proper protection of a natural space, as you maintain. The Courel has an unusual forest of its own: the cascades of chestnut trees. It turns out, at least reconstructive, not to say suspicious, that the Union dislodges these bumps, while the brown processing industry imports into Galicia tons of this fruit. All of this calls for the motion put forward. I want to remind you that neither mining nor the wind industry nor the woodland create stable and sufficient work in the Courel, and we will not discuss, please, that these are activities of high impact for habitats, which is what we intend to preserve and protect and on which we intend to base the development and improvement of the greenhouse. Mining, for example, not only destroys the environment, but also damages the quality of life of the inhabitants, as many, many cases of silicosis are being discovered in the resident population. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, all recognize the deterioration of the Courel, also the mayor of the People's Party, who was able to speak at the national convention of that party claiming desperate help, at the national convention! I've been looking for the initiatives presented about Coruel, and you don't have any. And, you know what? Whatever the matter, they have that opportunity, which they can take advantage of by voting in favour regardless of who promoted the initiative. That's why I want to speak positively. If what we want is to save The Courel from the dramatic situation in which he finds himself in terms of the population and the conservation of his architectural, monumental, cultural, agricultural, linguistic, landscape, ecological heritage, we must act immediately. And these are the proposals: to carry out a plan to improve the basic services of the population of this place and the infrastructure. This plan must respond to reality and not be limited to common obribs, without taking into account that Courel is a greenhouse and that, since it is considered a mountain area, should be protected by article 130.2 of the Constitution and the 1982 Mountain Agriculture Act, which developed it. It is obvious that a mountainous area requires differentiated treatment of other lands, such as those of the Quiroga Valley. Second proposed measure: a employment plan aimed at the conservation and recovery of the natural and cultural heritage, including the recovery of all villages; a employment plan with future projection for the environment and its inhabitants, which can generate an exciting project so that corals are not forced to emigrate or see their future black. These measures, these proposals in a positive way, would be amplified if they complement the natural park declaration for O Courel. The natural park declaration would make it essential to collaborate with the inhabitants since the development of the Rector Plan itself, making environmental conservation compatible with other types of forest, water and tourist uses consensual and valued through their participation in the park's advisory meeting. So far all the protections they stand for have gone to the back of their neighbours. The Natura Network Management Plan has been managed from offices; it is done by technocrats who interpret plans, but do not interpret needs or involve the population. And this same affected population is the one that considers abusive and indiscriminate the current protection imposed on the Courel. So sooner or later, you should start the relevant procedures to declare this singular, magical and emblematic greenhouses as a natural park, which, in addition to the economic, ecological and demographic advantages, would not only increase the generic restrictions of the Natura Network for the neighbors, but would realize them, and adjust territories perfectly delimited with small priority habitats, and complement that statement with improvements in infrastructure and services so that choosing to live in the Courel is attractive and a realistic option for people of all ages. I hope and wish you to approve this initiative. Nothing else. Thank you very much.

People of Galicia, at the initiative of Da Paula Prado del Río and eight other Members, on the impetus for the Galician Union to extend the family mediation programmes
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u190	This motion for a non-law amendment was tabled by the Joint Parliamentary Group and the Parliamentary Group of the Socialists of Galicia.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u191	To formulate the non-law proposal has the word donna Paula Prado del Río.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u192	Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, good afternoon. Well, let's talk about mediation. Mediation is a process in which everyone wins. By its very nature, a mediation can only be concluded with a solution that is beneficial to all sides. All those who make use of it, but also those who don't use it. Those who use it because, in addition to saving emotional costs in family affairs, they also avoid the rigidity of the judicial process. And also because mediation itself relieves the judges and the judicial institutions, so that other people can benefit from the agility of their processes in which they are immersed. And that's why we all win when we talk about mediation, and we think it's important to continue to promote these kinds of initiatives, which are no longer alternatives to the judicial route, to encourage agility in conflict resolution – I insist – for those who agree, but also for those who remain on the judicial track, which remains uncongested. In this sense, I would like to highlight the offer of mediation activity in resolving family conflicts in cases of marital or couple crisis. This institution, based on the understanding of the procedural parts, has an important public utility, since it represents for families and for units of stable living with residence in the territory of Galicia an important measure to resolve their conflicts; above all, in those cases where these couples or marriages have children or daughters, which in the event of family conflict require suitable consensus solutions to the problems that arise from the couple's crisis that go beyond the judgment itself that can reach a judicial process in which they are immersed. Because children and children should never suffer – even if they suffer but at least suffer less – those situations that can happen in the marital crises. European Directive 2008/52 of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union considers that intra-judicial mediation, i.e. that which leads to the end when the judicial process is already under way, is also an appropriate and valid methodology for solving a significant percentage of judicial processes, particularly in those cases where both parties will continue to be connected in the future because of their common interests, which is perfectly adapted to cases of family breakup – especially in cases where there are children or minor children –. This directive has been transposed into our legislation under Law 5/2012. In Galicia, before this transposition was made, in 2009, the Galician Union initiated a pilot process of intrajudicial mediation in the family by signing an agreement at the Santiago de Compostela Judiciary Party, a collaboration agreement with the College of Lawyers, and this project was extended to the Justice Party of Ourense from 2011, Vigo in December 2012, in Lugo in 2013, and last year, 2014, extended to the Pontevedra Judiciary Party. Well, the intrajudicial family mediation instrument has a real contrasting effect, as it shows the fact that, from the beginning of this experience I'm reporting in Galicia, from the year 2009 until the end of last year, the family judges of Santiago de Compostela, Ourense, Vigo and Pontevedra have resulted in mediation over 1,000 issues. And with regard to those couples who effectively agreed to attempt mediation beyond the first initial session, more than half of these families, specifically 56%, ended the mediation process with an agreement. We therefore believe that it is an important instrument, that it is an effective instrument and that it is also having proper results in the relations of paternal-maternal-filial conflict. Well, ladies and gentlemen, in family court proceedings, the family relationship does not end with the judgment, but in the case of children and daughters in common relations will remain beyond the judicial resolution, so the solution reached with the consent of the parties will ensure a better future relationship and compliance with the sentence. The Galician Union has invested more than half a million euros in these projects, and we consider it important that this conflict resolution system be extended to other judicial parties. This is the object of this non-law proposal. That is why we are presenting this initiative, in order to achieve the support of the rest of the groups, because we believe that it is good for everyone, it is far from political ideology, because I believe that this is the first time we have talked about mediation and I believe that we all agree, that there is more mediation and that there are more alternatives to conflict resolutions than the judicial route, for better for all. In this sense, I would like in turn to see the honourable Members who are in favour of the amendments, both of the Socialist Party and of the Joint Group, explain the amendment a little bit. In the case of the Socialist Party, which asks to enable specific rooms, we would like to be told where these specific rooms were located, and, instead of providing these offices with staff and economic means, which staff would be and which would be those economic means, because it seems to us to be a little generic and we would like it to be more concrete what the final claim of that amendment is. Like the Joint Group, which says, "It will provide places for the holding of meetings, as well as the budgetary departure it needs to allow the recruitment of an accredited group of mediation professionals." Well, we believe that the accredited mediation professional groups are currently working with lawyers and psychologists, and it seems to us enough, I don't know if there's any more professionals to intervene, according to the Joint Group, in these mediation processes. Nothing more and thank you very much.
