ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u194	Thank you, President. This initiative, which was registered almost a year ago, urges Together to promote, implement and develop actions in the area of de-judiciaryization of conflicts; in particular to extend intra-judiciary family mediation programmes. This honourable Member is not unaware of the usefulness of family mediation: safeguarding personal relationships, diminishing emotional costs and the parties themselves assume the role of the solution in cases of rupture of coexistence, precisely, except in cases of macho violence, because mediation requires that the relationships between the members of the couple be equal, and male violence is the most brutal manifestation of inequality between the couples. If there is violence, the risk to the victim is increased and the principles that govern equality stand out. And so it is expressed by representatives of justice and feminist associations, who insist that family mediation is not the right tool in cases of male violence. Therefore, it is appropriate to be alert that the outcome of this mediation does not without judicial rejection leave the harassment, insults and all the ill-treatment that does not entail physical aggression. What would have been the opposite effect of the mediator spirit and greater impunity of the aggressor and greater suffering on the part of the victim. And if this mediation is applied torturingly and by unskilled people, and motivated by doctrinal and religious principles, that can be the cause that brought as a result the women's obligation to close at home and assume the role of victim in the future. It must not therefore be a generic measure of application, since it would then be contrary to the comprehensive law of gender violence and assumed a clear retreat. That's why it's very important to have very clear what kind of conflict is advisable to mediation and how far free will and autonomy can come from the parties' will. This second aspect, in my view, is of vital importance, because the will of the parties is the basis of any extrajudicial method. If not necessarily binding, except on the part of the parties' will, it would not be advisable to mediate when reaching an agreement is not mandatory. On the other hand – and now I'm going to the amendment, Mrs. Paula – mediating is a trade, even if it's not exclusive; if, as a profession it is, it's necessary to learn its technique and foundations in practice and theory. That is why the amendment is concerned, because we are concerned about the formation of minimums which cover the laws in that field and the consequences that may have the agreements reached in that mediation. The role of mediator must be exercised from impartiality, independence, training and professional experience. And that, of course, implies an budget to pay these kinds of services to professionals, as you said, of law and psychology. I also find it important to have neutral spaces where mediation flows in a relaxed and pleasant, comfortable environment for its realization, because this can influence the outcome of it. Popular of Galicia, at the initiative of Mr Miguel Ángel Santalices Vieira and seven other Members, on the actions to be carried out by the Government of Greece in connection with the programme Conecta 72
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u218	This proposal was amended by the Joint Group, the AGE, the Nationalist Block of Galicia and the Socialist Group.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u219	To formulate the non-law proposition, Mr. Santalices Vieira has the floor.
ParlaMint-ES-GA_2015-06-09-DSPG112.u220	Thank you, Madam President. Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. We bring here today a subject that I think is interesting, which is referred to a program for the care of those patients who receive hospital discharge. In particular, this is a special program within the first 72 hours of hospital discharge, in which they will receive from primary care teams, through the nursing service, a call to assess the patient's health status. This is what is known as Program Connect 72, which is 72 hours after discharge. We must not confuse – because there is some confusion – Connect 72 with home hospitalization, there are two different things. Home hospitalization is a program in which patient follow-up to the medical requirements of the hospital is valued. But this is a much more specific program, it's about making a phone call to those patients who received a hospital discharge. Automaticly from generating a hospital discharge, it is generated in the schedule of the Primary Care Service, through nursing, a demand to contact the patient and evaluate his status. So it's also a program that allows better coordination between primary nursing and hospital nursing, which was pursued with integrated management areas. It's a program that, in addition, is well valued by citizenship, because it brings a lot of benefits, at least from what this program has implemented, because it's a program that has specific objectives. In short, we could say that it's about improving the patient's care quality after a discharge, a discharge that occurs in the hospital. It increases the relationship and trust of patients and caregivers in health care. It also facilitates, and axillizes, the integration and continuity of patient care after hospital discharge. And it also serves to prevent and intervene early in the face of health problems that may arise after a hospital discharge. So I think it's a very valuable program and its implementation is beneficial for the whole citizenship. We have to frame it within the chronic patient care strategy, which will benefit from this, but not only chronic patients. They're going to be beneficiaries of this program all those who receive a discharge. There will undoubtedly be more incidence in chronic patients because the chronic patient generates more hospital stays than the other patients. But – I insist – the program aims to address all patients with a hospital episode, which at 72 hours can be called to assess their health status. This, too, – and we will discuss it later – reinforces the role of nursing within the National Health System, because the fundamental role passes through primary care nursing. And here too, technology is of great importance, because in Galicia, fortunately, we have the Ianus program, which allows it to be, in some way, already directly affected by the results and evaluation of patients. Galicia was a pioneer here in this sense, and therefore I think we are in a position to carry this program forward throughout the autonomous community. Well, predictable calculations point to that in Galicia there are 230,000 hospital highs that can occur over a year; so we're talking about a program that has an important content. It's 230,000 people, and even though some can be repetitive episodes of hospital income, it has its benefits. There are also some matters under which the Connect 72 program should not be applied, given the specificities of a hospital service. So, and in short, Madam President, I think it is an interesting initiative, which benefits very much from citizenship and population after hospital admission, and calls for support from all Members and Members for this non-legislative proposal. Nothing else, Madam President.

And the progressive closure of agricultural holdings is emptying the lands they used to occupy, making visible one of the grounds for fires, that one year yes and another tear our mountains as well. The birth and growth of these abandoned farms of spontaneous vegetation, and the deterioration of productive infrastructures – research, roads, water systems, etc. – are also a consequence. It is such a state of deterioration that it is very difficult to recover agricultural activity in the near future. Accelerating demographic depression and the devastating effects of agricultural crises, linked to fires, are some of the main factors that can explain the existence of more than 200,000 empty homes in rural Galicia, 1,300 depopulated nuclei – approximately 50 per cent of the entire state – 30 white villages a year remain empty and 750 have only one inhabitants. Slowly, person by person, Galicia remains empty until it looks like a museum of shadows or a green desert. The policies of budget cuts and services that apply to the Union and the Council are not adequate for this dramatic situation to change. The rural environment budget is reduced by 15 million euros. It turns rural childhood schools into units to cut expenses, so one teacher has to attend, for example, 14 girls of different ages and educational levels in the same class; in this way, families with school-aged children leave in search of quality education. Health centres are also closed and health services restricted. These cuts, coupled with the abandonment of rural development policies and programmes, prevent our villages from staying alive. You have the opportunity, the Government of the Union, to collaborate and work to build an alternative, sustainable development model that provides quality of life for the population, and frees the population from depopulation. Policies that bet on agricultural vocational training, on decent employment in the agricultural sector, on the bet on the value of ours. Policies that enhance agricultural and livestock family farms. I'll support the initiative. Thank you.
